<file path=".agents/skills/skill-router/AGENTS.md">
# AGENTS.md — skill-router

## What this repo is

**skill-router** is a pure SKILL.md routing skill. It routes user prompts to the
best matching installed skill. No Python, no install, no build.

The primary artifact is `SKILL.md` at the repo root. Copy it to any agent
platform's skills directory and it works.

## Repo structure

```
SKILL.md          ← the product (copy this to ~/.claude/skills/skill-router/)
README.md         ← usage instructions
cli/              ← optional Python CLI (parser, registry, TF-IDF matching)
  pyproject.toml  ← pip installable (pip install cli/)
  types.py        ← shared data types
  scanner/        ← cross-platform skill directory scanner
  matcher/        ← (future) TF-IDF matcher
  composer/       ← (future) multi-skill orchestration
  network/        ← (future) skills.sh search
  tests/          ← 36 tests ✅
```

## Important

- The SKILL.md IS the product. Everything else is optional.
- The Python CLI is an optional companion, not the main tool.
- The routing table in SKILL.md is self-maintaining — add new rules as you discover them.
</file>

<file path=".agents/skills/skill-router/README.md">
# skill-router

A pure SKILL.md router for agent skills. Install it like any other skill.
Your AI reads the routing rules and automatically loads the right skill for
whatever the user asks.

```bash
# Install: copy SKILL.md to your skills directory
cp SKILL.md ~/.claude/skills/skill-router/SKILL.md

# Or use npx skills
npx skills add Liber1917/skill-router
```

## How it works

This is a meta-skill. It doesn't do work itself — it routes user intent to the
best matching installed skill.

```
User: "帮我写一篇论文综述"
  → skill-router reads routing table
  → Matches "论文" to arxiv-reader + "综述" to content-ops
  → pipeline: arxiv-reader → content-ops
  → Loads both, executes in sequence
```

## What it covers

- **50+ routing rules** — writing, code, research, design, data, workflow, more
- **Multi-skill patterns** — pipeline, parallel, compose
- **8+ platforms** — Claude Code, WorkBuddy, OpenCode, Gemini CLI, Codex etc.
- **Network fallback** — `npx skills find` when nothing local matches
- **Self-maintaining** — add new rules as you discover them

## Quick start

1. Copy `SKILL.md` to your skills directory
2. Start asking your AI to do things without naming a skill
3. The AI reads this file and routes automatically

## CLI (optional)

A Python CLI tool lives in `cli/` for power users who want TF-IDF matching
or batch routing table maintenance. Purely optional — the SKILL.md works alone.

```bash
pip install cli/   # or: cd cli && pip install -e .
skill-route "帮我写论文"
```

## Design

Why a SKILL.md and not a Python package? Because GSD proved that routing is
fundamentally an AI-interpretable task, not a computational one. The AI reads
the routing rules, understands intent, and makes better routing decisions than
any TF-IDF vector could. The SKILL.md is self-contained, zero-install, and
works across every agent platform.

## License

MIT
</file>

# Skill Router — LIFECYCLE (MUST FOLLOW)

**CRITICAL: This is a meta-skill. It does NOT do work itself. It routes.**

**HUMAN-IN-THE-LOOP: Every routing decision requires user confirmation.**
skill-router does NOT auto-execute anything. It proposes, user disposes.
At every decision point — load a skill, run a pipeline, install from network —
skill-router presents the plan and waits for explicit user approval.

```
User: "帮我写论文"
  → skill-router: 检测到 paper-reading (0.92)，加载吗？
  → User: 加载
  → skill-router: 加载 paper-reading，开始工作
  → skill-router: 完成，结果满意吗？
  → User: 满意 / 不满意（重新路由）
```

**Every output line from this skill MUST be prefixed with `[skill-router]`.**
This makes the audit trail grep-able across sessions.
`grep '\[skill-router\]'` in any transcript to see what fired.

```
[skill-router] ┌─ ENTRY GUARD ─→ SCAN ─→ MATCH ─→ LOAD GUARD ─→ Load skill ─→ VERIFY HOOK ─→ CONTROL HOOK ─┐
[skill-router] └────────────────────────── persistent loop ────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

Works across Claude Code, WorkBuddy, OpenCode, Gemini CLI, Codex, OpenClaw,
Cursor, and any platform that uses `SKILL.md`.

---

## 1. ENTRY GUARD — ROUTE OR CHAT?

**FIRST thing you do when this skill is loaded.** Before any scan, any match —
decide: does this user message need routing at all?

If the user is clearly just chatting:
- Greetings ("你好", "hello")
- Simple factual questions ("Python 怎么排序", "今天天气")
- Casual conversation
→ Exit router. Do NOT prefix anything. Reply normally.

If the user describes a task, names a domain, or asks "帮" + verb:
→ Enter SCAN phase.

**Rule**: When in doubt, route. False positives waste 5 seconds. False
negatives miss the right skill entirely.

---

## 2. SCAN — DISCOVER ALL INSTALLED SKILLS

**This is mandatory. You MUST NOT skip this phase.**

Read every `SKILL.md` you can find. Extract **name**, **description**, and
**triggers** from YAML frontmatter.

Check these locations **in order**:

| Priority | Level | Paths |
|----------|-------|-------|
| **1st** | **Project** | `.claude/skills/*/SKILL.md`, `.workbuddy/skills/*/SKILL.md`, `.opencode/skills/*/SKILL.md`, `.gemini/skills/*/SKILL.md`, `.codex/skills/*/SKILL.md`, `.openclaw/skills/*/SKILL.md`, `.cursor/skills/*/SKILL.md`, `.agents/skills/*/SKILL.md` |
| **2nd** | **User** | `~/.claude/skills/*/SKILL.md`, `~/.workbuddy/skills/*/SKILL.md`, `~/.config/opencode/skills/*/SKILL.md`, `~/.gemini/skills/*/SKILL.md`, `~/.codex/skills/*/SKILL.md`, `~/.openclaw/skills/*/SKILL.md`, `~/.agents/skills/*/SKILL.md` |

**Rules**:
- Same-name skills: project-level wins over user-level.
- Missing dir: skip silently.
- No frontmatter: dir name as fallback.
- **Do NOT scan** hidden dirs (`.git/`, `__pycache__/`, etc.).
- Report: `[skill-router] Scanned {N} skills across {M} platforms: {list}.`

---

## 3. MATCH — COMPARE AGAINST USER INTENT

Compare the user's request against each scanned skill's **name**, **description**,
and **triggers**.

| Level | Condition | Action |
|-------|-----------|--------|
| **High (≥0.85)** | Exact trigger match or unambiguous semantic match | Load immediately. **Do NOT ask.** |
| **Medium (0.5–0.85)** | Semantic match, not exact | Show ≥2 options with scores. Let user pick. |
| **Low (<0.5)** | Multiple partial matches | Show all. Ask clarifying questions. |
| **No match** | Nothing relevant | Go to network fallback. |

### Cross-Domain Detection (run after scoring)

Before presenting results, check for multi-intent signals:

**Linguistic signals** (user expresses ≥2 intents):
- Chinese: "又...又...", "既要...又要...", "和", "还有", "然后", "之后"
- English: "and also", "plus", "then", "after that", "first...then"

**Candidate signals** (matches span different domains):
- ≥2 candidates with scores in [0.5, 0.85] range
- OR ≥2 High candidates from different domains
- Candidate `tags` have NO intersection → different domains

**Decision**:
- `cross_domain = true` → BOTH signal types present
- `cross_domain = possible` → only linguistic signal detected
- Record candidate list for Section 4

**CRITICAL**: Do NOT invent skills. Do NOT guess. If not found → fallback.

---

## 4. LOAD GUARD — VERIFY BEFORE LOADING

After match, **do NOT load anything yet**. Pass through this guard.
**Every path requires user confirmation**.

| Match | Guard action |
|-------|-------------|
| **High** | Announce + ask once: `[skill-router] 匹配到 {skill} ({score})，加载这个 skill 吗？` |
| **Medium** | Present options with scores. User MUST pick. |
| **Medium + cross_domain=true** | Present options + pipeline/compose suggestion (Section 9). |
| **Low** | Ask clarifying. Re-match. |
| **None** | Network fallback. |

**User declines high match** → Ask: "要不要试试其他候选？" or exit routing.
If user says no to all: exit gracefully. `[skill-router] Control: no skill selected — user declined.`

If error during loading: call VERIFY HOOK → failure path.

---

## 5. NETWORK FALLBACK

If no installed skill matches:

1. `npx skills find "<describe need in 5-10 English words>"`
2. Present up to 3 options with name + description + install command.
3. User confirms → `npx skills add <owner/repo@skill> -g -y`
4. After install, skill is immediately available.

If `npx` not available → suggest skills.sh or GitHub.

---

## 6. VERIFY HOOK — CHECK EXECUTION RESULT

After the loaded skill finishes, **ask the user for feedback**:

`[skill-router] {skill} 执行完成。结果满意吗？`
- User confirms (✅) → Move to CONTROL HOOK.
- User rejects (❌ or "不行", "不对") → `[skill-router] Unloading {skill}. 重新匹配...`
  → Return to SCAN for next candidate.
  → If 2 consecutive failures: ask user to clarify their request.

**Success**: Skill ran without errors (user will decide relevance).
**Failure**: Skill errored or produced empty output.
→ `[skill-router] Verify: ❌ {skill} — execution error`
→ Unload. Return to SCAN for next candidate.

**CRITICAL**: The user, not the system, decides whether the result is acceptable.
Do NOT skip the satisfaction check even if the skill "looks correct."

---

## 7. CONTROL HOOK — PERSISTENT SESSION WITH BOUNDARY GUARD

After route + execution, the router stays active — but **does not re-route silently**.
A BOUNDARY GUARD runs on each new user message:

```
New message
    │
    ▼
┌──────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  BOUNDARY GUARD                          │
│  Match msg ↔ current skill domain        │
├──────────────────────────────────────────┤
│  ≥0.85  → stay silent, pass thru         │
│  <0.85  → is user asking for a skill?    │
│    ├ YES (帮/找/推荐skill/which/路由) → ask│
│    └ NO → stay silent, pass thru         │
└──────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Critical: BOUNDARY GUARD is silent by default.** It only speaks when
the user literally asks for routing help. This avoids wasting context
turns on "do you need a skill?" noise in long-running sessions.

**Rules**:
- **≥0.85 (same thread)** → Stay silent. Pass through. Never mention routing.
- **<0.85 + user asks for skill** ("帮我找 sdkill", "推荐 skill", "有XX技能吗", "which skill", "路由") → Ask once: `[skill-router] 需要我帮你找合适的 skill 吗？`
  - Confirms → Enter SCAN → MATCH → LOAD GUARD.
  - Declines → Pass through. This session's BOUNDARY GUARD is suppressed.
- **<0.85 + user does NOT ask for skill** → Stay silent. Pass through. Do NOT ask.
- **CRITICAL**: Do NOT re-route automatically. Do NOT ask unprompted. The BOUNDARY GUARD is a **listener with a hotkey**, not a greeter.

---

## 8. OUTPUT FORMAT — GREP-ABLE CONTRACT

Every route step **MUST** prefix with `[skill-router]`. Example:

```
[skill-router] Entry: wants routing — task detected
[skill-router] Scan: 46 skills across 3 platforms
[skill-router] Match: paper-reading (high ~0.92) — trigger "论文" matches
[skill-router] Load Guard: Passed (≥0.85)
[skill-router] Verify: ✅ paper-reading completed correctly
[skill-router] Control: waiting — thread active
```

**Failure format**:
```
[skill-router] Match: no match found
[skill-router] Fallback: searching skills.sh...
[skill-router] Found "thesis-writer" (write academic papers). Install?
```

**Multi-skill format**:
```
[skill-router] Entry: wants routing — multi-domain
[skill-router] Scan: 46 skills
[skill-router] Match: pipeline detected — paper-reading → thesis-writing
[skill-router] Load Guard: Proposing pipeline to user
```

---

## 9. MULTI-SKILL ROUTING

**When to propose** (triggered from Section 4 when `cross_domain=true`):

Present user with a concise choice **in 1 round**:

```
[skill-router] 检测到多领域需求，请选择执行方式：
  1. Pipeline（顺序执行）：A 完成后，将结果传给 B
  2. Compose（组合执行）：A 和 B 同时使用，各自处理任务的不同部分
  3. 单独执行某个技能（{list individual skills}）
```

**Patterns**:

| Pattern | When to use | Execution |
|---------|--------------|-------------|
| **Pipeline (A → B)** | User says "先...再...", "然后", "step by step" | Load A → run → capture output → load B with output → VERIFY at end |
| **Compose (A + B)** | User says "同时", "一起", domains are independent | Load both → run together → merge outputs → VERIFY |
| **Parallel (A ∥ B)** | User wants independent results merged | Load both → run independently → merge → VERIFY |

**Execution rules**:
1. Execute ONE skill at a time (no parallel API calls)
2. Pass context explicitly: "A 的输出是：{output}. 现在用 B 处理。"
3. After all skills run → VERIFY HOOK with combined output
4. If user picks "单独执行" → fall back to normal single-skill load

**CRITICAL**: Never auto-execute multi-skill without user confirmation. Always present the plan first.

---

## 10. LOGGING (OPT-IN)

If the user has given explicit consent:
Append a JSONL line to `~/.skill-router/log.jsonl` after each route:

```jsonl
{"ts":"2026-05-07T09:00:00","prompt":"帮我写论文","matched":"paper-reading","score":0.92,"verify":"success"}
```

**Do NOT log without asking the user first.**
When asked, explain: "This logs routing outcomes locally to measure accuracy.
No data leaves your machine. Opt in?"

---

## 11. COMPLIANCE CHECKLIST

| # | Check | Pass |
|---|-------|------|
| 1 | Did I pass ENTRY GUARD (route or chat)? | ☐ |
| 2 | Did I SCAN actual directories (not guess)? | ☐ |
| 3 | Did I compare against ALL scanned skills? | ☐ |
| 4 | Did I ask user before loading (even high match)? | ☐ |
| 5 | Did I prefix every output with `[skill-router]`? | ☐ |
| 6 | Did I ask user satisfaction after skill execution? | ☐ |
| 7 | Did I run BOUNDARY GUARD before re-routing or staying silent? | ☐ |
| 8 | Did I get user confirmation before any external action (install/network)? | ☐ |

**If any box is unchecked, you skipped a step. Fix it.**
</file>

"""
Autonomous Agent OS Package for JARVIS 2.0
Provides 3-Tier OS interaction:
- Tier 1: Dynamic Python & Shell Code Synthesizer (code_executor)
- Tier 2: Model Context Protocol Client Hub (integrations.mcp_client)
- Tier 3: Set-of-Mark Vision & Accessibility GUI Agent (state_machine, som_vision, ui_distiller, os_actuator)
Protected by the Safety Interceptor gatekeeper.
"""

from .safety_interceptor import SafetyInterceptor, safety_interceptor
from .code_executor import CodeExecutor, code_executor
from .ui_distiller import UIDistiller, ui_distiller
from .som_vision import SOMVision, som_vision
from .os_actuator import OSActuator, os_actuator
from .state_machine import OSAgentStateMachine, os_agent

__all__ = [
    "SafetyInterceptor",
    "safety_interceptor",
    "CodeExecutor",
    "code_executor",
    "UIDistiller",
    "ui_distiller",

<file path="agent_os/code_executor.py">
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Tier 1 — Dynamic Code & Shell Synthesizer for JARVIS 2.0
Synthesizes self-contained Python and shell scripts on the fly for file management,
data processing, PDF tasks, media conversions, and OS diagnostics.
Executes scripts inside isolated subprocesses with timeout protection and safety checks.
"""

import os
import subprocess
import sys
import tempfile
import threading
import time
from typing import Any, Callable, Dict, List, Optional

from agent_os.safety_interceptor import safety_interceptor
from core.llm_router import router


class CodeExecutor:
    """
    Tier-1 Autonomous OS Agent: Code Synthesizer and Subprocess Runner.
    """

    def __init__(self):
        self._lock = threading.Lock()
        self._step_listeners: List[Callable[[Dict[str, Any]], None]] = []

    def register_step_listener(self, callback: Callable[[Dict[str, Any]], None]) -> None:
        """Register a callback for AGENT_STEP events."""
        with self._lock:
            if callback not in self._step_listeners:
                self._step_listeners.append(callback)

    def _emit_step(
        self,
        step: int,
        thought: str,
        action: str,
        status: str = "running",
        output: str = "",
        screenshot_b64: str = "",
    ) -> None:
        """Broadcast AGENT_STEP event to frontend."""
        payload = {
            "step": step,
            "tier": "code",
            "thought": thought,
            "action": action,
            "status": status,
            "screenshot_b64": screenshot_b64,
            "output": output,
        }
        with self._lock:
            listeners = list(self._step_listeners)
        for cb in listeners:
            try:
                cb(payload)
            except Exception as e:
                print(f"[CodeExecutor] Step listener error: {e}")

    def synthesize_script(self, task_description: str) -> str:
        """
        Prompts the LLM to write a clean, self-contained Python script to accomplish the task.
        """
        system_prompt = (
            "You are an expert Python OS automation engineer for JARVIS.\n"
            "Write a single, self-contained, robust Python 3 script to accomplish the user's task.\n"
            "Rules:\n"
            "1. Output ONLY executable Python code inside a ```python ``` block. No conversational filler.\n"
            "2. Use standard libraries (os, sys, pathlib, json, shutil, subprocess, math, re, csv, etc.) or popular libraries.\n"
            "3. Print meaningful output to stdout so the user can understand the results.\n"
            "4. Handle errors cleanly with try/except.\n"
        )
        prompt = f"User Request: {task_description}"
        raw = router.query_text("intent_router", prompt, system_prompt=system_prompt)

        # Extract code from markdown block
        code = raw.strip()
        if "```python" in code:
            code = code.split("```python")[1].split("```")[0].strip()
        elif "```" in code:
            code = code.split("```")[1].split("```")[0].strip()

        return code

    def execute_task(self, task_description: str, timeout_sec: int = 30) -> Dict[str, Any]:
        """
        Full Tier-1 Pipeline:
        1. Synthesize script from user description.
        2. Pass code through SafetyInterceptor.
        3. Execute script in subprocess with timeout.
        4. Capture stdout/stderr and return structured result.
        """
        self._emit_step(
            step=1,
            thought=f"Synthesizing Python automation script for: '{task_description}'",
            action="synthesize_code",
            status="running",
        )

        code = self.synthesize_script(task_description)
        if not code:
            self._emit_step(
                step=1,
                thought="Failed to synthesize script",
                action="synthesize_code",
                status="error",
                output="LLM returned empty code.",
            )
            return {"ok": False, "summary": "Failed to synthesize script for task.", "output": ""}

        # Safety Check
        is_safe, risk_reason = safety_interceptor.inspect_code(code)
        if not is_safe:
            self._emit_step(
                step=2,
                thought=f"Safety warning: {risk_reason}. Requesting user confirmation.",
                action="safety_intercept",
                status="running",
                output=code,
            )
            approved = safety_interceptor.request_approval(
                action_type="python_code_execution",
                description=f"{risk_reason} in synthesized task: '{task_description}'",
                preview=code[:400] + ("..." if len(code) > 400 else ""),
                timeout_sec=15,
            )
            if not approved:
                self._emit_step(
                    step=2,
                    thought="Operation was aborted by safety gatekeeper or user denial.",
                    action="safety_intercept",
                    status="aborted",
                    output="Execution denied by user safety policy.",
                )
                return {
                    "ok": False,
                    "summary": "Execution was denied by safety policy, sir.",
                    "output": "Aborted by user.",
                }

        self._emit_step(
            step=2,
            thought="Code verified safe. Launching runner subprocess.",
            action="execute_subprocess",
            status="running",
            output=code,
        )

        # Write to temporary file and execute
        with tempfile.NamedTemporaryFile(suffix=".py", mode="w", encoding="utf-8", delete=False) as f:
            f.write(code)
            tmp_path = f.name

        try:
            start_t = time.time()
            proc = subprocess.run(
                [sys.executable, tmp_path],
                capture_output=True,
                text=True,
                timeout=timeout_sec,
            )
            duration_ms = int((time.time() - start_t) * 1000)

            stdout = proc.stdout.strip()
            stderr = proc.stderr.strip()
            returncode = proc.returncode

            combined_output = stdout
            if stderr:
                combined_output = f"{combined_output}\n[STDERR]: {stderr}".strip()

            if returncode == 0:
                summary = stdout or "Script executed successfully with zero errors."
                self._emit_step(
                    step=3,
                    thought="Script completed successfully.",
                    action="finish",
                    status="done",
                    output=combined_output,
                )
                return {
                    "ok": True,
                    "summary": summary,
                    "output": combined_output,
                    "duration_ms": duration_ms,
                    "code": code,
                }
            else:
                summary = f"Script returned exit code {returncode}."
                self._emit_step(
                    step=3,
                    thought=f"Script failed with exit code {returncode}.",
                    action="finish",
                    status="error",
                    output=combined_output,
                )
                return {
                    "ok": False,
                    "summary": f"{summary} Details: {stderr or stdout}",
                    "output": combined_output,
                    "duration_ms": duration_ms,
                    "code": code,
                }

        except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
            self._emit_step(
                step=3,
                thought=f"Script timed out after {timeout_sec}s.",
                action="finish",
                status="error",
                output=f"TimeoutExpired ({timeout_sec}s)",
            )
            return {
                "ok": False,
                "summary": f"Script execution timed out after {timeout_sec} seconds, sir.",
                "output": "TimeoutExpired",
            }
        except Exception as e:
            self._emit_step(
                step=3,
                thought=f"Subprocess runner exception: {e}",
                action="finish",
                status="error",
                output=str(e),
            )
            return {
                "ok": False,
                "summary": f"Subprocess runner error: {e}",
                "output": str(e),
            }
        finally:
            try:
                if os.path.exists(tmp_path):
                    os.unlink(tmp_path)
            except OSError:
                pass


# Global code executor singleton
code_executor = CodeExecutor()

<file path="agent_os/os_actuator.py">
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
OS Actuator for JARVIS 2.0
Executes low-level mouse, keyboard, and window controls via PyAutoGUI and pynput.
Includes an emergency [ESC] hardware kill-switch and instant programmatic abort.
"""

import threading
import time
from typing import List, Optional, Union

try:
    import pyautogui
    pyautogui.FAILSAFE = True  # Move mouse to corner to abort
    pyautogui.PAUSE = 0.05
    PYAUTOGUI_AVAILABLE = True
except Exception:
    PYAUTOGUI_AVAILABLE = False


class OSActuator:
    """
    Direct OS actuator controlling mouse clicks, keyboard keystrokes, and hotkeys.
    Provides an emergency [ESC] kill-switch to immediately halt automated actions.
    """

    def __init__(self):
        self._lock = threading.Lock()
        self._aborted: bool = False
        self._esc_listener = None
        self._start_esc_listener()

    def _start_esc_listener(self) -> None:
        """Starts a background pynput listener monitoring for the [ESC] key."""
        try:
            from pynput import keyboard

            def on_press(key):
                if key == keyboard.Key.esc:
                    print("\n🚨 [OSActuator] EMERGENCY [ESC] KILL-SWITCH TRIGGERED! Halting autonomous agent.")
                    self.abort()

            self._esc_listener = keyboard.Listener(on_press=on_press)
            self._esc_listener.daemon = True
            self._esc_listener.start()
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"[OSActuator] Emergency key listener notice: {e}")

    # ── Abort Controls ────────────────────────────────────────────────
    def abort(self) -> None:
        """Trigger emergency abort."""
        with self._lock:
            self._aborted = True

    def is_aborted(self) -> bool:
        """Check if abort kill-switch has been triggered."""
        with self._lock:
            return self._aborted

    def reset_abort(self) -> None:
        """Reset the abort flag before starting a new autonomous task."""
        with self._lock:
            self._aborted = False

    # ── Actuator Actions ──────────────────────────────────────────────
    def click(self, x: int, y: int) -> bool:
        """Left click at screen coordinates (x, y)."""
        if self.is_aborted():
            return False
        if not PYAUTOGUI_AVAILABLE:
            print(f"[OSActuator Mock] Click at ({x}, {y})")
            return True
        try:
            pyautogui.click(x, y)
            return True
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"[OSActuator] Click error: {e}")
            return False

    def double_click(self, x: int, y: int) -> bool:
        """Double click at screen coordinates (x, y)."""
        if self.is_aborted():
            return False
        if not PYAUTOGUI_AVAILABLE:
            print(f"[OSActuator Mock] Double-click at ({x}, {y})")
            return True
        try:
            pyautogui.doubleClick(x, y)
            return True
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"[OSActuator] Double-click error: {e}")
            return False

    def right_click(self, x: int, y: int) -> bool:
        """Right click at screen coordinates (x, y)."""
        if self.is_aborted():
            return False
        if not PYAUTOGUI_AVAILABLE:
            print(f"[OSActuator Mock] Right-click at ({x}, {y})")
            return True
        try:
            pyautogui.rightClick(x, y)
            return True
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"[OSActuator] Right-click error: {e}")
            return False

    def type_text(self, text: str, interval: float = 0.02) -> bool:
        """Type string into focused element."""
        if self.is_aborted():
            return False
        if not PYAUTOGUI_AVAILABLE:
            print(f"[OSActuator Mock] Type text: '{text}'")
            return True
        try:
            pyautogui.typewrite(text, interval=interval)
            return True
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"[OSActuator] Type text error: {e}")
            return False

    def press_hotkey(self, keys: Union[List[str], str]) -> bool:
        """Press key combination (e.g. ['ctrl', 'c'] or 'enter')."""
        if self.is_aborted():
            return False
        if isinstance(keys, str):
            key_list = [k.strip() for k in keys.split("+")]
        else:
            key_list = keys

        if not PYAUTOGUI_AVAILABLE:
            print(f"[OSActuator Mock] Hotkey: {key_list}")
            return True
        try:
            pyautogui.hotkey(*key_list)
            return True
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"[OSActuator] Hotkey error: {e}")
            return False

    def scroll(self, amount: int) -> bool:
        """Scroll mouse wheel (positive up, negative down)."""
        if self.is_aborted():
            return False
        if not PYAUTOGUI_AVAILABLE:
            print(f"[OSActuator Mock] Scroll: {amount}")
            return True
        try:
            pyautogui.scroll(amount)
            return True
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"[OSActuator] Scroll error: {e}")
            return False


# Global actuator singleton
os_actuator = OSActuator()
</file>

<file path="agent_os/safety_interceptor.py">
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Safety Interceptor & Risk Gatekeeper for JARVIS 2.0
Inspects synthesized code and operating system actions before execution.
Triggers HUD Approval Modal events for destructive operations when Autonomous Trust Mode is OFF.
"""

import re
import threading
import time
import uuid
from typing import Any, Callable, Dict, List, Optional, Tuple

from config.settings import get_vault


class SafetyInterceptor:
    """
    Evaluates risks in scripts and actions. Intercepts dangerous operations
    and prompts for approval via the Holographic HUD Approval Modal.
    """

    DESTRUCTIVE_PATTERNS = [
        r"\brm\s+-rf\b",
        r"\brmdir\s+/[sS]\b",
        r"\bdel\s+/[fFqQsS]\b",
        r"\bdel\s+[*]\b",
        r"\bformat\s+[a-zA-Z]:",
        r"\bshutil\.rmtree\b",
        r"\bos\.remove\b",
        r"\bos\.unlink\b",
        r"\bos\.rmdir\b",
        r"\bshutdown\s+",
        r"\btaskkill\s+/[fF]\b",
        r"\bdrop\s+table\b",
        r"\bdrop\s+database\b",
        r"\bdd\s+if=",
        r"\bmkfs\b",
    ]

    def __init__(self):
        self._lock = threading.Lock()
        self._pending_requests: Dict[str, Dict[str, Any]] = {}
        self._listeners: List[Callable[[Dict[str, Any]], None]] = []

    def register_approval_listener(self, callback: Callable[[Dict[str, Any]], None]) -> None:
        """Register a callback for APPROVAL_REQUIRED events."""
        with self._lock:
            if callback not in self._listeners:
                self._listeners.append(callback)

    def is_trust_mode_active(self) -> bool:
        """Check if Autonomous Trust Mode is enabled in settings."""
        try:
            vault = get_vault()
            return bool(vault.get("autonomous_trust_mode", False))
        except Exception:
            return False

    def inspect_code(self, code: str) -> Tuple[bool, str]:
        """
        Inspect code for risky or destructive patterns.
        Returns: (is_safe: bool, risk_description: str)
        """
        if not code:
            return True, ""

        for pattern in self.DESTRUCTIVE_PATTERNS:
            match = re.search(pattern, code, re.IGNORECASE)
            if match:
                return False, f"Potentially destructive operation detected: '{match.group(0)}'"

        return True, ""

    def request_approval(
        self,
        action_type: str,
        description: str,
        preview: str,
        timeout_sec: int = 15,
    ) -> bool:
        """
        Pauses and requests user approval via HUD ApprovalModal.
        If Autonomous Trust Mode is active, bypasses modal and auto-approves.
        """
        if self.is_trust_mode_active():
            print(f"[SafetyInterceptor] Autonomous Trust Mode active: Auto-approved '{action_type}'")
            return True

        req_id = f"req-{uuid.uuid4().hex[:8]}"
        event_obj = threading.Event()

        payload = {
            "request_id": req_id,
            "action_type": action_type,
            "description": description,
            "preview": preview,
            "timeout_sec": timeout_sec,
        }

        with self._lock:
            self._pending_requests[req_id] = {
                "event": event_obj,
                "approved": False,
                "timestamp": time.time(),
            }
            listeners = list(self._listeners)

        # Notify UI listeners
        for cb in listeners:
            try:
                cb(payload)
            except Exception as e:
                print(f"[SafetyInterceptor] Listener error: {e}")

        print(f"[SafetyInterceptor] Approval required ({req_id}): {description}. Waiting up to {timeout_sec}s...")

        # Wait for user input or timeout
        signaled = event_obj.wait(timeout=float(timeout_sec))

        with self._lock:
            req_data = self._pending_requests.pop(req_id, None)

        if not signaled:
            print(f"[SafetyInterceptor] Request {req_id} timed out. Defaulting to DENIED.")
            return False

        if req_data and req_data.get("approved"):
            print(f"[SafetyInterceptor] Request {req_id} was APPROVED by user.")
            return True
        else:
            print(f"[SafetyInterceptor] Request {req_id} was DENIED by user.")
            return False

    def approve(self, request_id: str) -> bool:
        """Approve a pending request."""
        with self._lock:
            req = self._pending_requests.get(request_id)
            if req:
                req["approved"] = True
                req["event"].set()
                return True
        return False

    def deny(self, request_id: str) -> bool:
        """Deny a pending request."""
        with self._lock:
            req = self._pending_requests.get(request_id)
            if req:
                req["approved"] = False
                req["event"].set()
                return True
        return False


# Global safety interceptor singleton
safety_interceptor = SafetyInterceptor()

<file path="agent_os/som_vision.py">
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Set-of-Mark (SoM) Vision Renderer for JARVIS 2.0
Captures desktop screenshots and overlays numbered Arc Cyan (#00E5FF) bounding boxes
and telemetry tags on interactive regions for Vision LLM consumption.
"""

import base64
import io
from typing import Any, Dict, List, Optional, Tuple
from PIL import Image, ImageDraw, ImageFont


class SOMVision:
    """
    Renders Set-of-Mark visual annotations on desktop screenshots.
    """

    ARC_CYAN = (0, 229, 255)
    BACKGROUND_DARK = (6, 10, 18, 220)
    TEXT_WHITE = (230, 247, 255)

    def capture_screen(self) -> Image.Image:
        """Captures primary desktop display image."""
        try:
            import mss
            with mss.mss() as sct:
                monitor = sct.monitors[1]
                sct_img = sct.grab(monitor)
                return Image.frombytes("RGB", sct_img.size, sct_img.bgra, "raw", "BGRX")
        except Exception:
            pass

        try:
            from PIL import ImageGrab
            return ImageGrab.grab().convert("RGB")
        except Exception:
            # Fallback synthetic canvas for headless environments
            return Image.new("RGB", (1280, 720), color=(10, 16, 26))

    def render_som(
        self,
        image: Image.Image,
        elements: List[Dict[str, Any]],
        box_color: Tuple[int, int, int] = ARC_CYAN,
    ) -> Tuple[Image.Image, str]:
        """
        Draws numbered cyan bounding boxes with high-contrast label badges.
        Returns: (annotated_pil_image, base64_png_string)
        """
        annotated = image.copy()
        draw = ImageDraw.Draw(annotated, "RGBA")

        # Load default font
        try:
            font = ImageFont.load_default()
        except Exception:
            font = None

        for el in elements:
            idx = el.get("id", 0)
            bbox = el.get("bbox", [0, 0, 0, 0])
            x, y, w, h = bbox

            if w <= 0 or h <= 0:
                continue

            # Draw outer Arc Cyan bounding box
            draw.rectangle(
                [x, y, x + w, y + h],
                outline=box_color + (255,),
                width=2,
            )

            # Draw label badge at top-left of box
            label_text = f"[{idx}]"
            badge_w = 26 + (len(str(idx)) * 6)
            badge_h = 16

            badge_x = max(0, x)
            badge_y = max(0, y - badge_h)

            draw.rectangle(
                [badge_x, badge_y, badge_x + badge_w, badge_y + badge_h],
                fill=self.BACKGROUND_DARK,
                outline=box_color + (200,),
                width=1,
            )

            draw.text(
                (badge_x + 4, badge_y + 2),
                label_text,
                fill=self.TEXT_WHITE,
                font=font,
            )

        # Encode to Base64 PNG
        buffered = io.BytesIO()
        annotated.save(buffered, format="PNG")
        b64_str = base64.b64encode(buffered.getvalue()).decode("utf-8")

        return annotated, b64_str

    def capture_and_annotate(self, elements: List[Dict[str, Any]]) -> Tuple[Image.Image, str]:
        """Convenience method: capture screen and overlay SoM boxes in one call."""
        img = self.capture_screen()
        return self.render_som(img, elements)


# Global SoM vision instance
som_vision = SOMVision()
</file>

<file path="agent_os/state_machine.py">
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Tier 3 — Autonomous OS Vision & Accessibility GUI Agent for JARVIS 2.0
Executes the Observe -> Think -> Act -> Verify loop using Multimodal Vision LLMs,
Set-of-Mark visual grounding, accessibility tree controls, and OS actuation.
"""

import json
import threading
import time
from typing import Any, Callable, Dict, List, Optional

from agent_os.os_actuator import os_actuator
from agent_os.som_vision import som_vision
from agent_os.ui_distiller import ui_distiller
from core.adapters.base import ChatMessage
from core.llm_router import router


class OSAgentStateMachine:
    """
    Tier-3 Autonomous Visual Operating System Agent.
    """

    def __init__(self):
        self._lock = threading.Lock()
        self._step_listeners: List[Callable[[Dict[str, Any]], None]] = []

    def register_step_listener(self, callback: Callable[[Dict[str, Any]], None]) -> None:
        """Register a callback for AGENT_STEP events."""
        with self._lock:
            if callback not in self._step_listeners:
                self._step_listeners.append(callback)

    def _emit_step(
        self,
        step: int,
        thought: str,
        action: str,
        status: str = "running",
        output: str = "",
        screenshot_b64: str = "",
    ) -> None:
        """Broadcast AGENT_STEP event to HUD."""
        payload = {
            "step": step,
            "tier": "vision_gui",
            "thought": thought,
            "action": action,
            "status": status,
            "screenshot_b64": screenshot_b64,
            "output": output,
        }
        with self._lock:
            listeners = list(self._step_listeners)
        for cb in listeners:
            try:
                cb(payload)
            except Exception as e:
                print(f"[OSAgent] Step listener error: {e}")

    def abort(self) -> None:
        """Immediately halt the running state machine."""
        os_actuator.abort()

    def run_task(self, task_description: str, max_steps: int = 10) -> Dict[str, Any]:
        """
        Executes the iterative Observe -> Think -> Act -> Verify autonomous loop.
        """
        os_actuator.reset_abort()
        print(f"\n[OSAgent] Commencing Tier-3 GUI autonomous task: '{task_description}'")

        history: List[str] = []

        for step_num in range(1, max_steps + 1):
            # Check for kill-switch
            if os_actuator.is_aborted():
                self._emit_step(
                    step=step_num,
                    thought="Autonomous execution halted by user abort / [ESC] kill-switch.",
                    action="abort",
                    status="aborted",
                    output="Emergency abort triggered.",
                )
                return {"ok": False, "summary": "Task aborted by user, sir.", "steps": step_num}

            # 1. OBSERVE: Distill active UI controls & capture SoM screenshot
            elements = ui_distiller.get_interactive_elements(max_elements=30)
            img, screenshot_b64 = som_vision.capture_and_annotate(elements)

            elements_summary = [
                f"[{el['id']}] {el['type']} '{el['name']}' at ({el['center'][0]}, {el['center'][1]})"
                for el in elements[:15]
            ]
            elements_text = "\n".join(elements_summary)

            self._emit_step(
                step=step_num,
                thought=f"Observing screen (Step {step_num}/{max_steps}). Found {len(elements)} interactive elements.",
                action="observe",
                status="running",
                screenshot_b64=screenshot_b64,
            )

            # 2. THINK: Vision LLM reasons over SoM screenshot and element coordinates
            system_prompt = (
                "You are JARVIS Tier-3 OS Vision Agent operating desktop applications visually like a human.\n"
                "You receive an image of the desktop screen with numbered Set-of-Mark cyan boxes [ID] and a list of elements.\n"
                "Return a JSON object in this exact schema:\n"
                "{\n"
                '  "thought": "<brief analysis of the active window and what to do next>",\n'
                '  "action": "click" | "double_click" | "type" | "hotkey" | "scroll" | "done",\n'
                '  "target_id": <int or null>,\n'
                '  "x": <int or null>,\n'
                '  "y": <int or null>,\n'
                '  "text": <string or null>,\n'
                '  "keys": <string or null>,\n'
                '  "done": <boolean>\n'
                "}\n"
            )

            user_prompt = (
                f"Goal: {task_description}\n"
                f"Current Step: {step_num} of {max_steps}\n"
                f"Previous steps: {'; '.join(history[-3:]) if history else 'None'}\n"
                f"Visible UI Elements:\n{elements_text}\n"
                "Analyze the screen and output your next action JSON."
            )

            messages = [
                ChatMessage(role="system", content=system_prompt),
                ChatMessage(role="user", content=user_prompt, image_b64=screenshot_b64),
            ]

            resp = router.dispatch_role("vision_agent", messages, json_mode=True)
            plan = resp.raw_json or {}

            thought = plan.get("thought", f"Proceeding with step {step_num}")
            action_name = plan.get("action", "click")
            is_done = bool(plan.get("done", False))

            history.append(f"Step {step_num}: {thought} -> {action_name}")

            # Check abort before acting
            if os_actuator.is_aborted():
                self._emit_step(
                    step=step_num,
                    thought="Execution aborted by user.",
                    action="abort",
                    status="aborted",
                    screenshot_b64=screenshot_b64,
                )
                return {"ok": False, "summary": "Task aborted by user, sir.", "steps": step_num}

            # 3. ACT: Execute the planned action
            act_output = ""
            target_id = plan.get("target_id")
            x = plan.get("x")
            y = plan.get("y")

            # Resolve coordinates from target_id if not explicitly provided
            if target_id is not None and (x is None or y is None):
                for el in elements:
                    if el.get("id") == target_id:
                        x, y = el["center"][0], el["center"][1]
                        break

            if action_name == "click" and x is not None and y is not None:
                os_actuator.click(x, y)
                act_output = f"Clicked at ({x}, {y})"
            elif action_name == "double_click" and x is not None and y is not None:
                os_actuator.double_click(x, y)
                act_output = f"Double-clicked at ({x}, {y})"
            elif action_name == "type" and plan.get("text"):
                if x is not None and y is not None:
                    os_actuator.click(x, y)
                    time.sleep(0.1)
                text_to_type = plan.get("text", "")
                os_actuator.type_text(text_to_type)
                act_output = f"Typed: '{text_to_type}'"
            elif action_name == "hotkey" and plan.get("keys"):
                hotkey_str = plan.get("keys", "")
                os_actuator.press_hotkey(hotkey_str)
                act_output = f"Pressed hotkey: {hotkey_str}"
            elif action_name == "scroll":
                amount = int(plan.get("y", -300) or -300)
                os_actuator.scroll(amount)
                act_output = f"Scrolled {amount}"
            elif is_done or action_name == "done":
                self._emit_step(
                    step=step_num,
                    thought=thought,
                    action="finish",
                    status="done",
                    output="Task successfully completed.",
                    screenshot_b64=screenshot_b64,
                )
                return {
                    "ok": True,
                    "summary": thought or "Task completed successfully, sir.",
                    "steps": step_num,
                }

            self._emit_step(
                step=step_num,
                thought=thought,
                action=action_name,
                status="running",
                output=act_output,
                screenshot_b64=screenshot_b64,
            )

            # 4. VERIFY: Pause briefly for GUI response
            time.sleep(0.6)

        return {
            "ok": True,
            "summary": f"Completed {max_steps} visual automation steps for: '{task_description}'",
            "steps": max_steps,
        }


# Global OS agent state machine singleton
os_agent = OSAgentStateMachine()
</file>

<file path="agent_os/ui_distiller.py">
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
UI Distiller for JARVIS 2.0
Extracts active OS window accessibility trees and interactive control coordinates (x, y, w, h).
Uses Windows UIAutomation / pywinauto when available, with a resilient fallback grid.
"""

import platform
from typing import Any, Dict, List, Optional


class UIDistiller:
    """
    Inspects desktop windows and extracts bounding boxes for interactive UI controls.
    """

    def __init__(self):
        self.os_type = platform.system()

    def get_interactive_elements(self, max_elements: int = 50) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
        """
        Extract interactive elements from the currently focused or active application window.
        Returns: list of {"id": int, "name": str, "type": str, "bbox": [x, y, w, h], "center": [cx, cy]}
        """
        elements: List[Dict[str, Any]] = []

        if self.os_type == "Windows":
            try:
                elements = self._extract_windows_uia(max_elements)
            except Exception as e:
                print(f"[UIDistiller] Windows UIA extraction notice: {e}")

        # If OS inspection returns few elements or is running in headless/fallback mode:
        if not elements:
            elements = self._generate_fallback_grid()

        return elements[:max_elements]

    def _extract_windows_uia(self, max_elements: int) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
        """Extract controls using comtypes / uiautomation or pywinauto."""
        extracted: List[Dict[str, Any]] = []

        try:
            import pywinauto
            from pywinauto import Desktop

            desktop = Desktop(backend="uia")
            active_window = desktop.active()
            if not active_window:
                return []

            ctrls = active_window.descendants()
            idx = 1
            for c in ctrls:
                try:
                    rect = c.rectangle()
                    width = rect.width()
                    height = rect.height()

                    # Filter out invisible or microscopic elements
                    if width < 12 or height < 12 or width > 3000 or height > 3000:
                        continue

                    name = c.window_text() or c.element_info.name or ""
                    ctrl_type = c.element_info.control_type or "Control"

                    cx = rect.left + width // 2
                    cy = rect.top + height // 2

                    extracted.append({
                        "id": idx,
                        "name": str(name)[:40],
                        "type": str(ctrl_type),
                        "bbox": [rect.left, rect.top, width, height],
                        "center": [cx, cy],
                    })
                    idx += 1
                    if len(extracted) >= max_elements:
                        break
                except Exception:
                    continue

        except Exception:
            pass

        return extracted

    def _generate_fallback_grid(self) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
        """
        Creates a clean 4x3 screen coordinate reference grid if accessibility APIs are restricted.
        """
        try:
            from PIL import ImageGrab
            screen = ImageGrab.grab()
            screen_w, screen_h = screen.size
        except Exception:
            screen_w, screen_h = 1920, 1080

        grid = []
        cols = 4
        rows = 3
        cell_w = screen_w // cols
        cell_h = screen_h // rows

        idx = 1
        for r in range(rows):
            for c in range(cols):
                x = c * cell_w + 20
                y = r * cell_h + 20
                w = cell_w - 40
                h = cell_h - 40
                grid.append({
                    "id": idx,
                    "name": f"Sector R{r+1}C{c+1}",
                    "type": "Region",
                    "bbox": [x, y, w, h],
                    "center": [x + w // 2, y + h // 2],
                })
                idx += 1

        return grid


# Singleton instance
ui_distiller = UIDistiller()

<file path="audio/__init__.py">

<file path="audio/tts_engine.py">
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Unified Text-to-Speech Engine for JARVIS 2.0
Supports edge-tts neural voices with pyttsx3 offline fallback.
Includes independent thread-safe tts_active state controls to allow silent GUI operation.
"""

import asyncio
import os
import queue
import tempfile
import threading
import time
from typing import Optional

try:
    import edge_tts
    EDGE_TTS_AVAILABLE = True
except ImportError:
    EDGE_TTS_AVAILABLE = False

try:
    import pygame
    PYGAME_AVAILABLE = True
except ImportError:
    PYGAME_AVAILABLE = False

try:
    import pyttsx3
    PYTTSX3_AVAILABLE = True
except ImportError:
    PYTTSX3_AVAILABLE = False

from config.settings import TTS_NEURAL_VOICE, TTS_FALLBACK_RATE, is_internet_connected


class TTSEngine:
    """
    Unified speech synthesis engine for JARVIS.
    Features:
    - Thread-safe independent tts_active toggle
    - edge-tts neural voice primary synthesis (when online)
    - pyttsx3 offline fallback (when offline or edge-tts fails)
    - Non-blocking background worker queue
    """

    def __init__(
        self,
        tts_active: bool = True,
        voice: str = TTS_NEURAL_VOICE,
        rate: int = TTS_FALLBACK_RATE,
        enabled: Optional[bool] = None,
        **kwargs,
    ):
        if enabled is not None:
            tts_active = enabled
        self._tts_active = bool(tts_active)
        self._lock = threading.Lock()
        self.voice = voice
        self.rate = rate

        # Audio queue and worker thread
        self._speech_queue: queue.Queue = queue.Queue()
        self._is_speaking = False

        # Initialize pygame mixer if available
        self._pygame_initialized = False
        if PYGAME_AVAILABLE:
            try:
                pygame.mixer.init()
                self._pygame_initialized = True
            except Exception as e:
                print(f"[TTS] pygame mixer init warning: {e}")

        # Start background worker thread
        self._worker_thread = threading.Thread(target=self._speech_worker, daemon=True, name="TTS-Worker")
        self._worker_thread.start()

    # ── State Controls ────────────────────────────────────────────────
    @property
    def tts_active(self) -> bool:
        with self._lock:
            return self._tts_active

    def get_tts_active(self) -> bool:
        """Return True if TTS speech output is active."""
        with self._lock:
            return self._tts_active

    def set_tts_active(self, active: bool) -> None:
        """Enable or disable TTS speech output."""
        with self._lock:
            self._tts_active = bool(active)
            if not self._tts_active:
                self.stop()

    def toggle_tts(self) -> bool:
        """Toggle TTS speech output state and return new state."""
        with self._lock:
            self._tts_active = not self._tts_active
            if not self._tts_active:
                self.stop()
            return self._tts_active

    # Aliases for backward compatibility
    def is_enabled(self) -> bool:
        return self.get_tts_active()

    def enable(self) -> None:
        self.set_tts_active(True)

    def disable(self) -> None:
        self.set_tts_active(False)

    def toggle(self) -> bool:
        return self.toggle_tts()

    def is_speaking(self) -> bool:
        """Check if audio playback is currently running."""
        return self._is_speaking

    def stop(self) -> None:
        """Stop current speech playback immediately."""
        if PYGAME_AVAILABLE and self._pygame_initialized:
            try:
                pygame.mixer.music.stop()
            except Exception:
                pass

    # ── Speech Ingestion ──────────────────────────────────────────────
    def speak(self, text: str, wait: bool = False) -> None:
        """
        Synthesize and speak text.
        If tts_active is False, skips audio synthesis completely and returns immediately.
        If wait is True, blocks until speech audio finishes.
        """
        if not text or not text.strip():
            return

        with self._lock:
            if not self._tts_active:
                return

        done_event = threading.Event() if wait else None
        self._speech_queue.put((text.strip(), done_event))

        if wait and done_event:
            done_event.wait()

    # ── Background Worker ─────────────────────────────────────────────
    def _speech_worker(self) -> None:
        """Worker thread processing queued speech items."""
        while True:
            try:
                item = self._speech_queue.get()
                if item is None:
                    break
                text, done_event = item

                with self._lock:
                    active = self._tts_active

                if active:
                    self._is_speaking = True
                    try:
                        self._play_speech(text)
                    except Exception as e:
                        print(f"[TTS Worker Error] {e}")
                    finally:
                        self._is_speaking = False

                if done_event:
                    done_event.set()
                self._speech_queue.task_done()
            except Exception as e:
                print(f"[TTS Loop Error] {e}")

    def _play_speech(self, text: str) -> None:
        """Synthesize and play speech via edge-tts (if online) or pyttsx3 fallback."""
        success = False
        online = is_internet_connected()

        if online and EDGE_TTS_AVAILABLE and PYGAME_AVAILABLE and self._pygame_initialized:
            try:
                success = self._synthesize_edge_tts(text)
            except Exception as e:
                print(f"[TTS] edge-tts error: {e}, falling back to pyttsx3")
                success = False

        if not success:
            self._synthesize_pyttsx3(text)

    def _synthesize_edge_tts(self, text: str) -> bool:
        """Synthesize via edge-tts and play via pygame."""
        with tempfile.NamedTemporaryFile(suffix=".mp3", delete=False) as f:
            tmpfile = f.name

        try:
            communicate = edge_tts.Communicate(
                text,
                voice=self.voice,
                rate="+25%",
                pitch="-5Hz",
            )
            asyncio.run(communicate.save(tmpfile))

            if os.path.exists(tmpfile) and os.path.getsize(tmpfile) > 0:
                pygame.mixer.music.load(tmpfile)
                pygame.mixer.music.play()
                while pygame.mixer.music.get_busy():
                    with self._lock:
                        if not self._tts_active:
                            pygame.mixer.music.stop()
                            break
                    time.sleep(0.04)
                return True
            return False
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"[TTS edge_tts] {e}")
            return False
        finally:
            try:
                if os.path.exists(tmpfile):
                    os.unlink(tmpfile)
            except OSError:
                pass

    def _synthesize_pyttsx3(self, text: str) -> None:
        """Offline fallback using pyttsx3."""
        if not PYTTSX3_AVAILABLE:
            return
        try:
            engine = pyttsx3.init()
            engine.setProperty("rate", self.rate)
            engine.say(text)
            engine.runAndWait()
            del engine
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"[TTS pyttsx3] {e}")


# Global TTS instance
tts_engine = TTSEngine()

<file path="audio/voice_listener.py">
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Thread-Safe Voice Listener & Concurrency Manager for JARVIS 2.0
Wraps SpeechRecognition in a background worker thread with:
- Instant mic_active toggle controls (set_mic_active, get_mic_active, toggle_mic)
- Meeting Auto-Mute Interlock (on_meeting_state_change) with manual override
- Push-to-Talk hotkey listener support
- Headless and permission-safe microphone fallback
"""

import os
import sys
import time
import threading
from typing import Callable, Optional

try:
    import speech_recognition as sr
    SR_AVAILABLE = True
except ImportError:
    SR_AVAILABLE = False

from config.settings import MIC_TIMEOUT, MIC_PHRASE_TIME_LIMIT, get_active_key


class VoiceListener:
    """
    Background worker managing microphone ingestion, meeting auto-mute,
    and voice command dispatching.
    """

    def __init__(
        self,
        on_command: Optional[Callable[[str], None]] = None,
        on_status_change: Optional[Callable[[bool, str], None]] = None,
        on_meeting_alert: Optional[Callable[[str, str], None]] = None,
    ):
        self.on_command = on_command
        self.on_status_change = on_status_change
        self.on_meeting_alert = on_meeting_alert

        # Thread synchronization
        self._lock = threading.Lock()
        self._active_event = threading.Event()
        self._stop_event = threading.Event()
        self._worker_thread: Optional[threading.Thread] = None
        self._ptt_listener = None

        # State flags
        self._mic_active: bool = False
        self.in_meeting: bool = False
        self.meeting_auto_muted: bool = False
        self.manual_override: bool = False

        # Speech recognition components
        self.recognizer = sr.Recognizer() if SR_AVAILABLE else None
        if self.recognizer:
            self.recognizer.energy_threshold = 4000
            self.recognizer.dynamic_energy_threshold = True
            self.recognizer.pause_threshold = 0.8

        # Start worker thread
        self.start()

    # ── State Controls ────────────────────────────────────────────────
    @property
    def mic_active(self) -> bool:
        with self._lock:
            return self._mic_active

    def get_mic_active(self) -> bool:
        """Return True if microphone is actively listening."""
        with self._lock:
            return self._mic_active

    def set_mic_active(self, active: bool) -> None:
        """Explicitly set mic listening state."""
        with self._lock:
            self._mic_active = bool(active)
            if self._mic_active:
                self._active_event.set()
                if self.in_meeting:
                    self.manual_override = True
                    self.meeting_auto_muted = False
            else:
                self._active_event.clear()

        if self.on_status_change:
            msg = "Microphone active" if active else "Microphone muted"
            self.on_status_change(active, msg)

    def toggle_mic(self) -> bool:
        """Toggle microphone state and return new state."""
        new_state = not self.get_mic_active()
        self.set_mic_active(new_state)
        return new_state

    # Aliases for backward compatibility
    def is_active(self) -> bool:
        return self.get_mic_active()

    def enable(self, manual_override: bool = False):
        self.set_mic_active(True)

    def disable(self, auto_meeting: bool = False):
        if auto_meeting:
            with self._lock:
                self.meeting_auto_muted = True
                self.manual_override = False
        self.set_mic_active(False)

    def toggle(self) -> bool:
        return self.toggle_mic()

    @property
    def auto_muted_by_meeting(self) -> bool:
        return self.meeting_auto_muted

    @auto_muted_by_meeting.setter
    def auto_muted_by_meeting(self, val: bool) -> None:
        self.meeting_auto_muted = val

    # ── Meeting Auto-Mute Interlock ───────────────────────────────────
    def on_meeting_state_change(self, in_meeting: bool, meeting_title: str = "Online Meeting") -> None:
        """
        Meeting Interlock:
        When in_meeting is True: automatically mute microphone (meeting_auto_muted = True),
        while allowing manual unmute override via set_mic_active(True).
        When in_meeting is False: restore mic listening if it was auto-muted and not overridden.
        """
        with self._lock:
            self.in_meeting = in_meeting

        if in_meeting:
            was_active = self.get_mic_active()
            if was_active:
                with self._lock:
                    self.meeting_auto_muted = True
                    self.manual_override = False
                self.set_mic_active(False)
                print(f"[AudioInterlock] Auto-muted microphone for meeting: {meeting_title}")
            else:
                with self._lock:
                    self.meeting_auto_muted = False
                    self.manual_override = False

            if self.on_meeting_alert:
                self.on_meeting_alert("joined", meeting_title)
        else:
            should_restore = False
            with self._lock:
                if self.meeting_auto_muted and not self.manual_override:
                    should_restore = True
                self.meeting_auto_muted = False
                self.manual_override = False

            if should_restore:
                print(f"[AudioInterlock] Meeting left ({meeting_title}). Restoring microphone.")
                self.set_mic_active(True)
            else:
                print(f"[AudioInterlock] Meeting left ({meeting_title}). Retaining microphone state.")

            if self.on_meeting_alert:
                self.on_meeting_alert("left", meeting_title)

    def handle_meeting_joined(self, meeting_title: str = "Online Meeting"):
        self.on_meeting_state_change(True, meeting_title)

    def handle_meeting_left(self, meeting_title: str = "Online Meeting"):
        self.on_meeting_state_change(False, meeting_title)

    # ── Push-To-Talk Hotkey Listener ──────────────────────────────────
    def start_push_to_talk(self, hotkey_str: str = "<ctrl>+<shift>+<space>") -> bool:
        """Registers global background hotkey listener for Push-to-Talk."""
        try:
            from pynput import keyboard

            def on_activate():
                print("[VoiceListener] Push-to-Talk hotkey triggered!")
                self.toggle_mic()

            self._ptt_listener = keyboard.GlobalHotKeys({
                hotkey_str: on_activate
            })
            self._ptt_listener.daemon = True
            self._ptt_listener.start()
            return True
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"[VoiceListener] Push-to-talk hotkey registration warning: {e}")
            return False

    def stop_push_to_talk(self) -> None:
        """Stops Push-to-Talk listener if active."""
        if self._ptt_listener:
            try:
                self._ptt_listener.stop()
            except Exception:
                pass
            self._ptt_listener = None

    # ── Thread Lifecycle ──────────────────────────────────────────────
    def start(self) -> None:
        """Start the background worker thread."""
        if self._worker_thread is None or not self._worker_thread.is_alive():
            self._stop_event.clear()
            self._worker_thread = threading.Thread(
                target=self._listen_worker,
                daemon=True,
                name="VoiceListener-Worker",
            )
            self._worker_thread.start()

    def stop(self) -> None:
        """Terminate the background worker thread."""
        self._stop_event.set()
        self._active_event.set()
        self.stop_push_to_talk()
        if self._worker_thread and self._worker_thread.is_alive():
            self._worker_thread.join(timeout=1.0)
        with self._lock:
            self._mic_active = False

    # ── Background Worker Loop ────────────────────────────────────────
    def _listen_worker(self) -> None:
        """Background worker looping while active."""
        while not self._stop_event.is_set():
            if not self._active_event.is_set() or not self.get_mic_active():
                # Sleep lightly when mic is inactive without touching microphone stream
                self._active_event.wait(timeout=0.3)
                continue

            if self._stop_event.is_set() or not self.get_mic_active():
                continue

            if not SR_AVAILABLE or not self.recognizer:
                time.sleep(0.5)
                continue

            try:
                # Only initialize sr.Microphone() when the user explicitly enables the mic
                with sr.Microphone() as source:
                    try:
                        self.recognizer.adjust_for_ambient_noise(source, duration=0.3)
                    except Exception:
                        pass

                    while self.get_mic_active() and not self._stop_event.is_set():
                        try:
                            audio = self.recognizer.listen(
                                source,
                                timeout=MIC_TIMEOUT,
                                phrase_time_limit=MIC_PHRASE_TIME_LIMIT,
                            )
                        except (sr.WaitTimeoutError, TimeoutError):
                            continue
                        except Exception as mic_err:
                            print(f"[VoiceListener] Mic read notice: {mic_err}")
                            time.sleep(0.5)
                            break

                        if audio and self.get_mic_active():
                            text = self._transcribe(audio)
                            if text and text.strip() and self.get_mic_active():
                                print(f"[VoiceListener] Spoken query: '{text}'")
                                if self.on_command:
                                    self.on_command(text.strip())

            except Exception:
                # Microphone unavailable or headless environment
                time.sleep(1.0)

    def _transcribe(self, audio) -> str:
        """Transcribe audio using Groq Whisper if key present, else Google STT."""
        groq_key = get_active_key("groq")
        if groq_key:
            try:
                from groq import Groq
                import tempfile
                client = Groq(api_key=groq_key)
                wav_bytes = audio.get_wav_data()
                with tempfile.NamedTemporaryFile(suffix=".wav", delete=False) as f:
                    f.write(wav_bytes)
                    tmp_name = f.name
                try:
                    with open(tmp_name, "rb") as f:
                        res = client.audio.transcriptions.create(
                            model="whisper-large-v3",
                            file=("audio.wav", f, "audio/wav"),
                            response_format="text",
                            language="en",
                        )
                    txt = res.strip() if isinstance(res, str) else res.text.strip()
                    if txt:
                        return txt
                finally:
                    if os.path.exists(tmp_name):
                        os.unlink(tmp_name)
            except Exception:
                pass

        try:
            return self.recognizer.recognize_google(audio).lower()
        except Exception:
            return ""


# Global voice listener singleton
voice_listener = VoiceListener()
